- **k**: Strategic advisor. Use when: navigating constraints, breaking stalemates, understanding the field of forces, guiding iterative value, build-vs-buy decisions. &lt;example&gt; Context: Team is stuck between competing concerns. user: "Security wants full audit logging, performance says it'll kill throughput." assistant: "I'll invoke K to find the strategic path through." &lt;commentary&gt; Competing forces creating stalemate. K finds the move that creates options. &lt;/commentary&gt; &lt;/example&gt; &lt;example&gt; Context: Architecture decision with many stakeholders. user: "Platform team wants standardization, product wan… ([yzavyas/claude-1337](https://github.com/yzavyas/claude-1337/blob/HEAD/plugins/arch-guild/agents/k.md) · ★8)
- **k-exaone**: Use when the main Claude thread wants a second pass from K-EXAONE 236B (LG AI Research), served by FriendliAI, via the opencode CLI — Korean-heavy prompts, Korean domain reasoning, long-context reading, or an independent opinion on a coding task. Do not use for trivial asks the main thread can finish on its own. ([kingsj0405/ClaudeCODEXaone](https://github.com/kingsj0405/ClaudeCODEXaone/blob/HEAD/plugins/exaone-friendli/agents/k-exaone.md) · ★3)
- **k-explorer**: Worker tier — task kinds `explore-search` and `summarize`. Use for read-only fan-out: find where something lives in code/docs, sweep many files, condense files/logs/docs into a brief. Returns conclusions, not file dumps. ([akumidv/alphavar](https://github.com/akumidv/alphavar/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/k-explorer.md) · ★7)
- **k-implementer**: Worker tier, mid rung — task kind `implement-under-spec`. Use to write code against a decided contract/design: the signatures, data shapes, and structure are already fixed; no open design decisions remain. Ships the change with its tests. ([akumidv/alphavar](https://github.com/akumidv/alphavar/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/k-implementer.md) · ★7)
- **k-mechanic**: Worker tier — task kinds `mech-edit`, `test-scaffold`, `doc-sync`. Use for mechanical, behaviour-preserving work from an explicit spec: renames, sweeps, formatting, moving code without reshaping it, test skeletons/fixtures from a stated spec, propagating an already-decided fact across docs. ([akumidv/alphavar](https://github.com/akumidv/alphavar/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/k-mechanic.md) · ★7)
- **k-reasoner**: Reasoner tier — task kinds `debug-deep`, `design-fork`, `quant-derivation`, `plan-draft`. Use for load-bearing thinking: multi-cause/cross-boundary failure analysis, drafting options for a contested design fork, deriving pricing/quant math, drafting a decomposition/zone plan for the orchestrator. Returns a structured report; it drafts, it never decides or edits. ([akumidv/alphavar](https://github.com/akumidv/alphavar/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/k-reasoner.md) · ★7)
- **k-validator**: Worker tier — task kind `validate-loop`. Use to drive project gates to green: run tests/lint/verification tools, parse the output, apply minimal mechanical fixes, re-run until green — or stop with a diagnosis when the failure is not mechanical. ([akumidv/alphavar](https://github.com/akumidv/alphavar/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/k-validator.md) · ★7)
- **k3-ask**: Use this agent when the user wants Kimi to answer a free-form question about the repository in prose — explain a module, trace a flow, compare alternatives, or reason about a concept in context. Choose this agent over k3-review when the user wants a narrative answer rather than diff-focused findings, and over k3-rescue when the user wants understanding rather than implementation. ([Brilia-it/k3-plugin-cc](https://github.com/Brilia-it/k3-plugin-cc/blob/HEAD/agents/k3-ask.md))
- **k3-challenge**: Use this agent when Claude wants Kimi to run an adversarial review that challenges the implementation approach, design choices, tradeoffs, and assumptions rather than just flagging defects. Choose this agent when the user wants pushback on whether the chosen approach is the right one, not a tighter pass over implementation defects (see k3-review for that). ([Brilia-it/k3-plugin-cc](https://github.com/Brilia-it/k3-plugin-cc/blob/HEAD/agents/k3-challenge.md))
- **k3-pursue**: Use this agent ONLY when the user has explicitly asked Kimi to pursue a stated objective AUTONOMOUSLY across multiple turns (experimental goal mode) — a multi-step task the user wants Kimi to drive to completion on its own, not a single bounded edit. This is the plugin's highest-autonomy surface: WRITE-CAPABLE, reusing the rescue trust boundary (the /k3:setup PreToolUse hook + workspace allowlist gate every tool call on every continuation turn; it cannot mutate git state), bounded by a MANDATORY hard --budget wall-clock ceiling. - **k3-rescue**: Use this agent proactively when the user wants to delegate a substantial investigation or implementation task to Kimi, especially multi-step bug hunts, cross-file refactors, or work the main Claude thread would rather offload than context-switch through. Choose this agent when the work is too large for an inline response but the user clearly wants it handed off, not just reviewed. - **k3-review**: Use this agent when Claude wants an independent second-pair-of-eyes review from Kimi over a working-tree diff or branch diff. Choose this agent for read-only diff review — multi-file changes, design-risk changes, or when the user explicitly asks for another reviewer. Not for implementation work (see k3-rescue) or free-form Q&A (see k3-ask). ([Brilia-it/k3-plugin-cc](https://github.com/Brilia-it/k3-plugin-cc/blob/HEAD/agents/k3-review.md))
- **k3-swarm**: Use this agent ONLY when the user has explicitly asked for a READ-ONLY review fanned out in PARALLEL across many independent targets (files, modules, or questions) at once — a user-scoped whole-directory or repo-wide audit where one subagent per target is the point. Requires BOTH signals: many independent targets AND an explicit request for parallel fan-out. Do NOT auto-promote a single, unscoped, or diff-shaped review into a per-file swarm — use k3-review for a single working-tree/branch diff or any review the user did not scope as a fan-out. Read-only (every subagent is hook-denied writes —… ([Brilia-it/k3-plugin-cc](https://github.com/Brilia-it/k3-plugin-cc/blob/HEAD/agents/k3-swarm.md))
- **k3-swarm-write**: Use this agent ONLY when the user has explicitly asked Kimi to make EDITS across MANY disjoint targets IN PARALLEL (a write fan-out) — e.g. "apply this same change to every handler, in parallel" or "fan out these independent edits across these N files." Requires BOTH signals: many independent WRITE targets AND explicit fan-out intent. WRITE-CAPABLE but PATCH-ONLY: edits happen in an ephemeral throwaway git worktree off HEAD and come back as a reviewable .patch — the plugin NEVER applies or commits, the user owns the merge, and the real working tree is never touched. - **kb-content-distiller**: Writes the Markdown KB files from extracted LaTeX content and taxonomy skeleton positions. Parallel-execution safe — one domain per instance. ([ave-veritas-et-enodatio/AVE-Core](https://github.com/ave-veritas-et-enodatio/AVE-Core/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/kb-content-distiller.md) · ★3)

Use when constructing or significantly revising a KB from LaTeX sources. ([ave-veritas-et-enodatio/AVE-Core](https://github.com/ave-veritas-et-enodatio/AVE-Core/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/kb-coordinator.md) · ★3)
- **kb-curation**: KB curation, enrichment, and citation specialist. Delegate when the task is to enrich entity/concept pages, repair citations and back-links, resolve contradictions, synthesize concepts, or maintain taxonomy, page templates, and resolver fixtures. Trigger phrases: "enrich this page", "fix citations", "audit provenance", "resolve this contradiction", "synthesize these concepts", "fix the filing / taxonomy", "update resolver fixtures". Not for retrieving answers (use kb-retrieval), ingesting new sources (use kb-ingestion), or read-only plugin/vault health audits (use kb-ops). - **knowledge-curator**: Operates inside the Open Knowledge Format knowledge base under kb/ — the read/write curator of a Karpathy-style LLM wiki. Use this agent for any task that centers on the knowledge base: answering questions from it, ingesting sources into it, initializing domains, keeping it healthy, or doing research whose findings should be captured for reuse.
